\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{p}.

(164)

Finally, by applying union over the
p-1
edges of the tree we get
\mathbb{P}\left[\left(\mathrm{E^{edge}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}})}\right)^{c}\right]\leq\delta
.
∎

The next Lemma is the extension of Lemma 8.7 by
Bresler and Karzand 2020
. The sample complexity bound exactly recovers the noiseless case and its expression is continuous at
q=0
. Further, the bound is independent of the length of the longest path
d
, similarly to the noiseless setting. Finally, we provide upper bounds on the functions that appear in the bound. The latter give a more tractable version of the result and a clear representation of the required number of samples as a function of the parameters.

Lemma 25

(Concentration bound for the event
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{cascade}}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}\right)
)
.

For
\beta>0
and
q\in[0,1/2)
we define the functions
S(\cdot)
,
G(\cdot)
,
K(\cdot)
,
A(\cdot)
,
\Delta(\cdot)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptS(\beta,q)

\displaystyle\triangleq 2+\frac{(1-2q)^{2}}{6}(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tanh^{2}(\beta)\leq 3-(1-2q)^{2}\triangleq S

(165)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptA(\beta,q)

\displaystyle\equiv A\triangleq(1-2q)^{2}[1-\tanh(\beta)(1-(1-2q)^{2})]

(166)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptG(\beta,q)

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{3}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\left[d(1-A)\left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)^{d}+1\right]\leq\frac{3\left(3e^{-1}\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}+1\right)}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\triangleq G,

(167)

and the inequality in (
167
) holds because the function
G(\beta,q)
is bounded for all
d\in\mathcal{N}\setminus\{1\}
.

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptK(\beta,q)

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{10(1-\tanh^{2}(\beta))}{9+(1-2q)^{2}-\tanh^{2}(\beta)(1-2q)^{2}(9(1-2q)^{2}+1)}\geq e^{-2\beta\mathds{1}_{q=0}}\triangleq K

(168)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\Delta

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{1-(1-2q)^{2}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\sqrt{\frac{3\log(2p^{3}/\delta)}{n}}\tanh^{2}(\beta)e^{2\beta}.

(169)

If
\Delta<\gamma_{\dagger}\leq S(\beta,q)G(\beta,q)/3+\Delta
and

\displaystyle n\geq\max\left\{\frac{S^{2}(\beta,q)G^{2}(\beta,q)}{0.3^{2}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}-\Delta\right)^{2}}\log(4p^{2}/\delta),\frac{108e^{2\beta}}{(1-2q)^{4}K(\beta,q)}\log(2p^{3}/\delta)\right\}

(170)

then for any path
\mathcal{A}_{d}=\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{d}\}
of
\mathrm{T}
with
d
edges, it is true that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\prod_{e\in\mathcal{A}_{d}}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e}}{(1-2q)^{2}}-\prod_{e\in\mathcal{A}_{d}}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{e}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right|\geq\gamma_{\dagger}\right)\leq\frac{2\delta}{p^{2}},\quad d>2.

(171)

Proof.

For sake of space we proceed by using the notation
\mu^{\dagger}_{k}
and
\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k}
instead of
\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{k}}
and
\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{k}}
for
k\in[d]
. Define the random variable

\displaystyle M^{\dagger}_{i}\triangleq\left(\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{i}}{(1-2q)^{2}}-\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{i}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)\prod^{i-1}_{j=1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=i+1}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}.

(172)

Then
\sum^{d}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}=\prod^{d}_{i=1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{i}}{(1-2q)^{2}}-\prod^{d}_{i=1}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{i}}{(1-2q)^{2}}
, and define the sequence of paths with length
k
as
\mathcal{A}_{k}\triangleq\left\{e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{k}\right\}\subset\mathcal{A}_{d}
, for
2\leq k\leq d
. Although we provided the definition of the event
\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\cdot)
in Lemma
24
, we restate it below for completeness. For some
\gamma_{e}>0
the definition follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{E^{edge}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k})}\triangleq\bigcap_{e\in\mathcal{A}_{k}}\left\{\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e}-\mu^{\dagger}_{e}\right|\leq\gamma_{e}\right\}.

(173)

The law of total probability gives

\displaystyle\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum^{d}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right|>\gamma\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum^{d}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right|>\gamma\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right)^{c}\right].

(174)

For second term, Lemma
24
gives that
\mathbb{P}\left[\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right)^{c}\right]\leq\delta/p^{2}
if

\displaystyle n\geq\frac{108e^{2\beta}\log(2p^{3}/\delta)}{(1-2q)^{4}K(\beta,q)},

(175)

we define the function
K(\beta,q)
in Lemma
24
(
157
). Here we will find an upper bound for the first term of the right hand-side of (
174
).
Note that
M^{\dagger}_{k}
is written as

\displaystyle M^{\dagger}_{k}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{\ell=1}\left(Y_{k}Y_{k+1}\right)^{(\ell)}}{(1-2q)^{2}}-\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{k}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{\ell=1}\left(\frac{\left(X_{k}N_{k}X_{k+1}N_{k+1}\right)^{(\ell)}}{(1-2q)^{2}}-\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{k}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}.

(176)

and we define

\displaystyle Z^{(\ell)}_{k}\triangleq\left(\frac{\left(X_{k}N_{k}X_{k+1}N_{k+1}\right)^{(\ell)}}{(1-2q)^{2}}-\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{k}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}.

(177)

The random variables
Z^{(\ell)}_{k}
for
\ell\in[n]
and fixed
k\in[d]
are independent conditioned on the event
\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})
. However the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}[Z^{(i)}_{k}|Z^{(i-1)}_{k},\ldots,Z^{(1)}_{k},\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1}]
is not zero. To apply a concentration of measure result on
Z^{(\ell)}_{k}
we use the extended Bennet’s inequality for supermartingales
(
Fan et al. 2012
)
.

Martingale Differences:
Define
\xi_{k}^{(0)}\triangleq 0
,
\xi_{k}^{(1)}\triangleq Z^{(1)}_{k}-\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(1)}_{k}|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1}\right]
,
\xi^{(i)}_{k}\triangleq Z^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|Z^{(i-1)}_{k},\ldots,Z^{(1)}_{k},\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1}\right]
. Also, define as
\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}
the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
Z^{(i-1)}_{k},\ldots,Z^{(1)}_{k},\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1}
, then
(\xi^{(i)}_{k},\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i})_{i=1.\ldots,n}
is a Martingale Difference Sequence (MDS).

Additionally, conditioned on
Z^{(i-1)}_{k},\ldots,Z^{(1)}_{k},\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!Z^{(i)}_{k}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\left(1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j},&\text{ w.p. }\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=+1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)\\
-\frac{1}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\left(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j},&\text{ w.p. }\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=-1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right),\end{cases}

(178)

and we have proved (Lemma
23
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=\pm 1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=\pm 1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\pm\mu^{\dagger}_{k}}{2}\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}+\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{1\pm\mu_{k}}{2}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}.

(179)

Thus we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|Z^{(i-1)}_{k},\ldots,Z^{(1)}_{k},\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=+1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)-(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=-1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2d}}.

(180)

Note that (
D
) gives

\displaystyle\left[\left(1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=+1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)-(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=-1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\left((1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k}}{2}-(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}}{2}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left((1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{1+\mu_{k}}{2}-(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{1-\mu_{k}}{2}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left((1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{1+\mu_{k}}{2}-(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{1-\mu_{k}}{2}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}+\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\mu_{k}-1+\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}+\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\mu_{k}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=(\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}.

(181)

Combine the latter with (
180
) to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]=\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}(\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2d}},\text{ }i\in[n].

(182)

If
q=0
then
\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]=0
. Also
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\to 0
for all
q\in[0,1/2)
because
\lim_{n\to\infty}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\to\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Z^{(i)}_{k}-\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Z^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]-\mathbb{E}^{2}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right].

(183)

We compute
\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(Z^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Big|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\Big]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Z^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Z^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}|Z^{(i-1)}_{k},\ldots,Z^{(1)}_{k},\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=+1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)+(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=-1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\right)^{2}.

(184)

We use (
D
) to find

\displaystyle\left[\left(1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=+1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)+(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(Y^{(\ell)}_{k}Y^{(\ell)}_{k+1}=-1\Big|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\left(1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)^{2}\frac{1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k}}{2}+\left(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)^{2}\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}}{2}\right)\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\left(1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)^{2}\frac{1+\mu_{k}}{2}+\left(1+\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)^{2}\frac{1-\mu_{k}}{2}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=(1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2})\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}+\left(1+(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}-2\mu_{k}\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=(1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2})\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}+\left(1+(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}-2(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}+2(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}-2\mu_{k}\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=(1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2})\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}+\left(1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k}-\mu_{k}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}\right)\left[\frac{1-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}+\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}\right]+2\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k}-\mu_{k}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}\right)+2\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k}-\mu_{k}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}.

(185)

Now we combine (
182
), (
183
), (
184
) and (
185
) to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Z^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]-\mathbb{E}^{2}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\left(1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k})^{2}\right)+2\mu^{\dagger}_{k}\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k}-\mu_{k}\right)\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}-\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}(\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}\right)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left[1-\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k}+\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}(\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}\right)^{2}\right]\left(\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\right)^{2}.

(186)

For sake of space we define the function

\displaystyle\hat{f}^{\mu_{k}}_{\mu_{k-1}}(q)\equiv f(\mu^{\dagger}_{k},\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1},\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1},q)\triangleq\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}(\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}},

(187)

and then (
182
) and (
186
) can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\hat{f}^{\mu_{k}}_{\mu_{k-1}}(q)\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2d}},\text{ }i\in[n],

(188)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[1-\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k}+\hat{f}^{\mu_{k}}_{\mu_{k-1}}(q)\right)^{2}\right]\left(\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\right)^{2},\text{ }i\in[n].

(189)

We would like to find an upper bound on the summation
\sum^{d}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\Big)^{2}\Big|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\Big]
. Define
A\equiv A(\beta,q)\triangleq(1-2q)^{2}[1-\tanh(\beta)(1-(1-2q)^{2})]
, for all
\beta>0
and
q\in[0,1)
. Then

\displaystyle\left[1-\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k}+\hat{f}^{\mu_{k}}_{\mu_{k-1}}(q)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[1-\left(\mu^{\dagger}_{k}+\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}(\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[1-\mu^{2}_{k}\left((1-2q)^{2}+\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}(1-(1-2q)^{2})\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left[1-\mu^{2}_{k}\left((1-2q)^{2}-|\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}|(1-(1-2q)^{2})\frac{|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}|}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left[1-\mu^{2}_{k}\left((1-2q)^{2}-(1-2q)^{2}\tanh(\beta)(1-(1-2q)^{2})\right)^{2}\right]

(190)

\displaystyle\leq[1-\mu^{2}_{k}A(\beta,q)],

(191)

and (
190
) holds because
|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}|\leq\gamma^{\dagger}_{j}\leq(1-2q)^{2}(1-\mu^{2}_{j})/6\leq(1-2q)^{2}(1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{j})^{2})/6
. Then (
189
) and (
191
) give

\displaystyle\sum^{d}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{d}_{k=1}[1-\mu^{2}_{k}A(\beta,q)]\left(\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-2q)^{2}}\sum^{d}_{k=1}[1-\mu^{2}_{k}A(\beta,q)]\prod^{d}_{j=1,j\neq k}\left[\mu_{j}^{2}+2\frac{\gamma^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right].

(192)

The inequality (
192
) holds under the event
\mathrm{E^{edge}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}})}
defined in (
158
) (see Lemma
24
) because

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{2}\leq\left(\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{2}+2\frac{\gamma^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}},

(193)

since
|\mu^{\dagger}_{j}|\leq(1-2q)^{2}
,
|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}|\leq(1-2q)^{2}
under the assumption of known
q
. Next we define
x_{j}\triangleq\mu_{j}^{2}+2\frac{\gamma^{\dagger}_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}
, then
3(1-A(\beta,q)x_{j})/2+(A(\beta,q)-1)/2)\geq 1-\mu^{2}_{k}A(\beta,q)
and (
192
) gives

\displaystyle\sum^{d}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-2q)^{2}}\sum^{d}_{k=1}\left[\frac{3}{2}(1-Ax_{j})+\frac{A-1}{2}\right]\prod^{d}_{j=1,j\neq k}x_{j}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{(1-2q)^{2}}\left[\frac{3}{2}(1-Ax)+\frac{A-1}{2}\right]x^{d-1}.

(194)

The latter is maximized at
x^{*}=(A+2)\left(1-\frac{1}{d}\right)
/3 and
A\in(0,1]
, thus we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{(1-2q)^{2}}\left[\frac{3}{2}(1-Ax)+\frac{A-1}{2}\right]x^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{(1-2q)^{2}}\left[\frac{3}{2}(1-A\frac{A+2}{3}+A\frac{A+2}{3d})+\frac{A-1}{2}\right]\left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)^{d-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{d}\right)^{d-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{(1-2q)^{2}}\left[\frac{3}{2}-\frac{A(A+2)}{2}+\frac{A-1}{2}\right](x^{*})^{d-1}+\frac{A(A+2)}{2(1-2q)^{2}}(x^{*})^{d-1}

\displaystyle=d\frac{2-A^{2}-A}{2(1-2q)^{2}}\left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)^{d-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{d}\right)^{d-1}+\frac{A(A+2)}{2(1-2q)^{2}}\left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)^{d-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{d}\right)^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq d\frac{2-A^{2}-A}{2(1-2q)^{2}}\left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)^{d-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\right)+\frac{A(A+2)}{2(1-2q)^{2}}\left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=d\frac{(A+2)(1-A)}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)^{d-1}+\frac{A(A+2)^{2}}{12(1-2q)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq d\frac{3(1-A)}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\left(\frac{A+2}{3}\right)^{d}+\frac{3}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\triangleq G(\beta,q)

(195)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-A)}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\frac{3}{e\log\frac{3}{A+2}}+\frac{3}{4(1-2q)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{3(1-A)}{e4(1-2q)^{2}\log\frac{3}{A+2}}+\frac{3}{4(1-2q)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\left(3e^{-1}+1\right)}{4(1-2q)^{2}}.

In (
195
) we define the function
G(\beta,q)
and we proved that it has an upper bound independent of
d\in[p-1]
,

\displaystyle G(\beta,q)\triangleq d\frac{3(1-A(\beta,q))}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\left(\frac{A(\beta,q)+2}{3}\right)^{d}+\frac{3}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\leq\frac{3\left(3e^{-1}\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}+1\right)}{4(1-2q)^{2}}\equiv G.

(196)

For the rest of the proof and the final result
G(\beta,q)
can be replaced by its upper bound in (
196
), however the definition of
G(\beta,q)
shows the continuity of the result for
q\to 0
.

The following inequality holds with probability
1
for all
i\in[n]
and
k\in[d]
under the event
\mathrm{E^{edge}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}})}
(Lemma
24
),

\displaystyle|\xi^{(i)}_{k}|

\displaystyle\leq 2+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=2+\frac{|\hat{f}^{\mu_{k}}_{\mu_{k-1}}(q)|}{(1-2q)^{2}}\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}|\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}|\mu^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2d-2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2+\frac{|\hat{f}^{\mu_{k}}_{\mu_{k-1}}(q)|}{(1-2q)^{2}}

\displaystyle=2+\frac{1}{(1-2q)^{2}}|\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}||(\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})|\frac{|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}|}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=2+\tanh^{2}(\beta)(1-(1-2q)^{2})\frac{\gamma^{\dagger}_{k-1}}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2+\frac{(1-2q)^{2}}{6}(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tanh^{2}(\beta)\triangleq S(\beta,q).

(197)

the last step comes form the inequality
\gamma^{\dagger}_{e}\leq\frac{(1-2q)^{2}}{6}(1-(\mu_{e})^{2})
(
161
), which holds if the inequality
n>\frac{108e^{2\beta}}{K(\beta,q)(1-2q)^{4}}\log(4p)
holds (see
159
). Recall that Lemma
24
gives

\displaystyle\gamma_{e}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{3\frac{1-\mu_{e}^{2}}{nK(\beta,q)}\log(2p^{3}/\delta)}\leq\sqrt{3\frac{1-\tanh^{2}(\beta)}{nK(\beta,q)}\log(2p^{3}/\delta)}\leq\sqrt{\frac{3\log(2p^{3}/\delta)}{n}}\equiv\tilde{\gamma}.

(198)

Also, for all
i\in[n]
we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum^{d}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum^{d}_{k=2}\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{d}_{k=2}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\sum^{d}_{k=2}\frac{|\hat{f}^{\mu_{k}}_{\mu_{k-1}}(q)|}{(1-2q)^{2}}\frac{\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}|\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}|\mu^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2d-2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-2q)^{2}}\sum^{d}_{k=2}|\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}|(\mu_{k}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k})\frac{|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}-\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1}|}{1-(\mu^{\dagger}_{k-1})^{2}}\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\frac{|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\frac{|\mu^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\tanh^{2}(\beta)(1-(1-2q)^{2})\frac{\gamma_{e}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\sum^{d}_{k=2}\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\frac{|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\frac{|\mu^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2}}

(199)

\displaystyle\leq\tanh^{2}(\beta)\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tilde{\gamma}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\sum^{d}_{k=2}\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\frac{|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\frac{|\mu^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2}}

(200)

\displaystyle\leq\tanh^{2}(\beta)\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tilde{\gamma}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\sum^{d}_{k=2}\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\frac{|\mu^{\dagger}_{j}|+\gamma_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\frac{|\mu^{\dagger}_{j}|}{(1-2q)^{2}}

(201)

\displaystyle=\tanh^{2}(\beta)\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tilde{\gamma}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\sum^{d}_{k=2}\prod^{k-1}_{j=1}\tanh(\beta)+\frac{\gamma_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\prod^{d}_{j=k+1}\tanh(\beta)

\displaystyle\leq\tanh^{2}(\beta)\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tilde{\gamma}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\sum^{d}_{k=2}\prod^{d}_{j=1,j\neq k}\tanh(\beta)+\frac{\gamma_{j}}{(1-2q)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\tanh^{2}(\beta)\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tilde{\gamma}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\sum^{d}_{k=2}\prod^{d}_{j=1,j\neq k}\left(\tanh(\beta)+\frac{1}{6}(1-\tanh^{2}(\beta))\right)

\displaystyle=\tanh^{2}(\beta)\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tilde{\gamma}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}(d-1)\left(\frac{5}{3}-\frac{1}{6}(\tanh(\beta)-3)^{2}\right)^{d-1}

\displaystyle\leq\tanh^{2}(\beta)\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tilde{\gamma}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\frac{1}{-e\log\left(\frac{5}{3}-\frac{1}{6}(\tanh(\beta)-3)^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tanh^{2}(\beta)\tilde{\gamma}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}e^{2\beta-1}.

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-(1-2q)^{2})\tanh^{2}(\beta)e^{2\beta}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\sqrt{\frac{3\log(2p^{3}/\delta)}{n}}\triangleq\Delta,

(202)

where (
199
), (
200
) , (
201
) come from Lemma
24
and (
198
). Finally,
0<\frac{5}{3}-\frac{1}{6}(\tanh(\beta)-3)^{2}<1
for all
\beta>0
and
-1/\log\left(\frac{5}{3}-\frac{1}{6}(\tanh(\beta)-3)^{2}\right)\leq e^{2\beta}
.

We use the symbol
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{A}_{k-1}}[\cdot]
to denote the conditional expectation given the the event
\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})
, for instance

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})\right]\equiv\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{A}_{k-1}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\right].

(203)

Further we define the function
F(\cdot,\cdot)
as

\displaystyle F(t,\lambda)=\log\left(\frac{1}{1+t}e^{-\lambda t}+\frac{t}{1+t}e^{\lambda}\right).

(204)

For any
k\leq d
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{A}_{k-1}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda M^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\Bigg|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right]\right]

(205)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{A}_{k-1}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}Z^{(i)}_{k}\right)\Bigg|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right]\right]

(206)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{A}_{k-1}}\Bigg[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)\exp\left(\lambda\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right)\Bigg|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right]\Bigg]

(207)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\lambda\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{A}_{k-1}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\frac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)\Bigg|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{k-1}\right]\right]

(208)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\lambda\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{A}_{k-1}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\exp\left\{n\;F\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]}{S(\beta,q)^{2}},|\lambda|\frac{S(\beta,q)}{n}\right)\right\}\right]

(209)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\lambda\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\left\{n\;F\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]}{S(\beta,q)^{2}},|\lambda|\frac{S(\beta,q)}{n}\right)\right\}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{A}_{k-1}}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\right].

(210)

The equation (
205
) comes from change of measure and tower property, the definitions (
176
) and (
177
) of
M^{\dagger}_{k}
and
\xi^{(i)}_{k}
respectively give (
206
) and (
207
). The (
208
) is derived by upper bounding the quantity
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right|
similarly to (
197
), (
209
) is the upper bound on the moment generating function of the supermartingale
Fan et al. 2012
. To get a recurrence we proceed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})\right]\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\cap\mathrm{E^{edge}_{e_{k-1},\dagger}}\right]\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\cap\mathrm{E^{edge}_{e_{k-1},\dagger}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\cap\mathrm{E^{edge}_{e_{k-1},\dagger}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{E^{edge}_{e_{k-1},\dagger}}\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{E^{edge}_{e_{k-1},\dagger}}}\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\right]\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k-1}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\right]\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-2})\right).

(211)

By applying the recurrence
d
times, we derive the following bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{d}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\left\{n\;\sum^{d}_{k=1}F\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]}{S(\beta,q)^{2}},|\lambda|\frac{S(\beta,q)}{n}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{d}_{k=1}\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{(i)}_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\left\{n\;dF\left(\frac{1}{d}\sum^{d}_{k=1}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi^{(i)}_{k}\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}^{k}_{i-1}\right]}{S(\beta,q)^{2}},|\lambda|\frac{S(\beta,q)}{n}\right)\right\}.

(212)

Further (
196
), (
197
), (
202
) and (
212
) give

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{k}_{i=1}M^{\dagger}_{i}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{k-1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\lambda\Delta(\beta,q)\right)\exp\left\{nd\;F\left(\frac{1}{d}\frac{G(\beta,q)}{S^{2}_{\xi}(\beta,q)},|\lambda|\frac{S(\beta,q)}{n}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{nd\;F\left(\frac{G(\beta,q)}{d},|\lambda|\frac{S(\beta,q)}{n}\right)+\lambda\Delta(\beta,q)\right\}.

(213)

For sake of space, we denote the functions
G(\beta,q),

S(\beta,q)
, and
\Delta(\beta,q)
as
G
,
S
, and
\Delta
respectively. It is true that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{d}_{k=1}M^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right]\mathbb{P}\left[\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\cap\mathrm{E}_{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{nd\;F\left(\frac{G}{d},|\lambda|\frac{S}{n}\right)+\lambda\Delta\right\}.

(214)

Under the assumption
n>\frac{108e^{2\beta}}{(1-2q)^{4}K(\beta,q)}\log(4p)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left(\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right)^{c}\right]\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(215)

The latter gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum^{d}_{k=1}M^{\dagger}_{k}\right)\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right]\leq 2\exp\left\{nd\;F\left(\frac{G}{d},|\lambda|\frac{S}{n}\right)+\lambda\Delta\right\},

(216)

which implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sum^{d}_{k=1}M^{\dagger}_{k}\geq\gamma\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\min_{\lambda>0}\exp\left\{nd\;F\left(\frac{G}{d},\lambda\frac{S}{n}\right)+\lambda\Delta-\lambda\gamma\right\}

\displaystyle=2\min_{\lambda>0}\exp\left\{nd\;F\left(\frac{G}{d},\lambda\frac{S}{n}\right)+\lambda\left(\Delta-\gamma\right)\right\},

(217)

and we define
\gamma^{\prime}\triangleq\gamma-\Delta
. The minimum value is attained at

\displaystyle\lambda^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{n/S}{1+G/d}\log\frac{1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}}{1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}}

(218)

and by substituting the optimal value we get

\displaystyle\exp\left\{nd\;F\left(\frac{G}{d},\lambda^{*}\frac{S}{n}\right)-\lambda^{*}\gamma^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{1}{1+\frac{G}{d}}\left(\frac{1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}}{1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}}\right)^{-\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}+\frac{\frac{G}{d}}{1+\frac{G}{d}}\left(\frac{1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{Gy}}{1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+G/d}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{Sd}\right)}\right]^{nd}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\frac{1}{1+\frac{G}{d}}\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)^{-\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)^{\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\frac{G}{d}}{1+\frac{G}{d}}\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+G/d}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{Sd}\right)}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)^{-\frac{1}{1+G/d}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{Sd}\right)}\Bigg]^{nd}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\frac{1}{1+\frac{G}{d}}\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)^{-\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)^{\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}-1}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\frac{G}{d}}{1+\frac{G}{d}}\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+G/d}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{Sd}\right)-1}\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)^{-\frac{1}{1+G/d}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{Sd}\right)}\Bigg]^{nd}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\frac{1}{1+\frac{G}{d}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)+\frac{\frac{G}{d}}{1+\frac{G}{d}}\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)\Bigg]^{nd}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left[\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)^{-\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)^{\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}-1}\right]^{nd}

\displaystyle=1^{nd}\times\left[\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)^{-\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)^{\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}-1}\right]^{nd}

\displaystyle=\left[\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)^{-\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)^{\frac{-1}{1+G/d}+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}\right]^{nd}.

(219)

Then (
217
) and (
219
) give

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sum^{d}_{k=1}M^{\dagger}_{k}\geq\gamma\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\left[\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)^{-\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dS}\right)^{\frac{-1}{1+G/d}+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}}\right]^{nd}.

(220)

As a final step we want to express the upper bound as an exponential function of
\gamma
, we define
\zeta\triangleq\gamma^{\prime}/(SG)
and we proceed as follows:

\displaystyle d\left[\left(\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{1+G/4d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dy)}{(1+G/4d)}\right)\log\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{dy}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq d\Bigg[\left(\frac{G/d}{1+G/d}+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}\right)\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}-\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(\frac{1}{1+G/d}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/(dS)}{(1+G/d)}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/dS}{1-\gamma^{\prime}/dS}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\Bigg[\left(\frac{dG}{d+G}+\frac{d\gamma^{\prime}/S}{(d+G)}\right)\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}-\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(\frac{d^{2}}{d+G}-\frac{d\gamma^{\prime}/S}{(d+G)}\right)\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/dS}{1-\gamma^{\prime}/dS}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/dS}{1-\gamma^{\prime}/dS}\right)^{2}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{d+G}\Bigg[\left(G+\gamma^{\prime}/S\right)\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}-\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(d-\gamma^{\prime}/S\right)\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/dS}{1-\gamma^{\prime}/dS}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}/dS}{1-\gamma^{\prime}/dS}\right)^{2}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d}{d+G}\left[\left(G+\gamma^{\prime}/S\right)\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}-\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right]^{2}\right)-\gamma^{\prime}/S\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{dG}{d+G}\left[\left(1+\gamma^{\prime}/(SG)\right)\left(\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}-\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{GS}\right]^{2}\right)-\gamma^{\prime}/(SG)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{dG}{d+G}\left[\left(1+\zeta\right)\left(\zeta-\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{2}\right)-\zeta\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2G}{2+G}\left[\left(1+\zeta\right)\left(\zeta-\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{2}\right)-\zeta\right]

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{3\gamma^{\prime}}{10GS}\right)^{2},\quad\forall\gamma^{\prime}\in(0,\frac{SG}{3}).

(221)

Recall that
\zeta\triangleq\gamma^{\prime}/(SG)
,
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma-\Delta
. If
\Delta<\gamma\leq S(\beta,q)G(\beta,q)/3+\Delta
then (
220
) and (
221
) give

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left[\sum^{d}_{k=1}M^{\dagger}_{k}\geq\gamma\Bigg|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-0.3^{2}n\frac{\left(\gamma-\Delta\right)^{2}}{S^{2}(\beta,q)G^{2}(\beta,q)}\right).

(222)

In a similar way we derive the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sum^{d}_{k=1}M^{\dagger}_{k}\leq-\gamma\big|\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{edge}}_{\dagger}(\mathcal{A}_{d-1})\right]\leq 2\exp\left(-0.3^{2}n\frac{\left(\gamma-\Delta\right)^{2}}{S^{2}(\beta,q)G^{2}(\beta,q)}\right).

(223)

Finally, we combine (
174
), Lemma
24
, (
222
) and (
223
) to derive the bound (
170
) which guarantees that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\prod^{d}_{i=1}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{i}}{(1-2q)^{2}}-\prod^{d}_{i=1}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{i}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right|>\gamma\right]\leq 2\delta/p^{2},\quad\forall d\geq 2.

(224)

To summarize we proved that the event
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{cascade}}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}\right)
happens with probability at least
1-2\delta/p^{2}
by combining Bresler’s and Karzand’s technique, the Corollary 2.3 by
Fan et al. 2012
and Lemma
23
.
∎

Appendix E
Predictive Learning, Proof of Theorem
3
and Theorem
7

Recall that our goal is to guarantee that the quantity
\mathcal{L}^{(2)}(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger}))
is smaller than a number
\eta>0
with probability at least
1-\delta
. To do this, we use the triangle inequality as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger})\right)\leq\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right)\right)+\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger})\right)

(225)

and we find the required number of samples such that each of the terms
\mathcal{L}^{(2)}(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right))
and
\mathcal{L}^{(2)}(\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger}))
in (
225
) is less than
\eta/2
with probability at least
1-\delta
. The next Lemma provides the necessary bounds on
\gamma_{\dagger}
and
\epsilon_{\dagger}
that guarantee

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(2)}(\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger}))\leq\eta/2.

Lemma 26
.

If
\gamma_{\dagger}\leq\frac{\eta}{3}
and

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\dagger}\leq(1-2q)^{2}e^{-\beta}\left[20\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)\right]^{-1},

then
\mathcal{L}^{(2)}(\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger}))\leq\eta/2
under the event
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{corr}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)\cap\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{cascade}}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}\right)\cap\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{strong}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)
.

Proof.

The derivation of the bound is similar to the approach by
Bresler and Karzand 2020
but with different calculations. In the hidden model, we consider the path between two nodes
i,j
in the estimated structure
\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}
, namely
\text{path}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(i,j)
, to be
(\mathcal{F}_{0},e_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1},e_{1}...,\mathcal{F}_{t-1},e_{t},\mathcal{F}_{t})
,
and
\mathcal{F}_{i}
are segments with all strong edges and
e_{i}

are all weak edges. We consider the case of at least one weak edge to exist in the path. If there is no weak edge the bound reduces to the case of Lemma
25
. The length of each sub-path
\mathcal{F}_{i}
is denoted as
d_{i}
, for all
i\in\{0,1,\ldots,t\}
. Each segment (sub-path)
\mathcal{F}_{i}
has exactly
d_{i}
edges, and the total number of edges in the path are
d
; thus
d=\sum^{t}_{i=0}d_{i}+t
. Note that
t\geq 1
and
d_{i}\geq 0
for all
i\in\{0,1,\ldots,t\}
. Recall that

\displaystyle\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\prod_{\left(i,j\right)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}}\frac{1+x_{i}x_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}X_{j}\right]}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\prod_{{\left(i,j\right)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}}}\frac{1+x_{i}x_{j}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{i}Y_{j}\right]}{\left(1-2q\right)^{2}}}{2}

(226)

(the latter comes from (
19
)), and

\displaystyle\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger})

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\prod_{\left(i,j\right)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}}\frac{1+x_{i}x_{j}\frac{\mathbb{\hat{E}}\left[Y_{i}Y_{j}\right]}{\left(1-2q\right)^{2}}}{2}.

(227)

Further, for any tree-structured Ising model distributions
P,\tilde{P}
with structures
\mathrm{T}=(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{E})
and
\tilde{\mathrm{T}}=(\mathcal{V},\tilde{\mathcal{E}})
respectively, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(P,\tilde{P}\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\sup_{i,j\in\mathcal{V}}\quad\frac{1}{2}\sum_{x_{i},x_{j}\in\{-1,+1\}^{2}}\left|P(x_{i},x_{j})-\tilde{P}(x_{i},x_{j})\right|

(228)

\displaystyle=\sup_{i,j\in\mathcal{V}}\quad\frac{1}{2}\left|\prod_{e\in\mathrm{path}_{\mathrm{T}}\left(i,j\right)}\mu_{e}-\prod_{e^{\prime}\in\mathrm{path}_{\tilde{\mathrm{T}}}\left(i,j\right)}\tilde{\mu}_{e^{\prime}}\right|.

(229)

To upper bound the quantity
\mathcal{L}^{(2)}(\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger}))
we have

\displaystyle 2\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger})\right)

\displaystyle=\left|\prod_{e\in\mathrm{path}_{\text{$\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}$}}\left(i,j\right)}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{e}}{\left(1-2q\right)^{2}}-\prod_{e\in\mathrm{path}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(i,j\right)}\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e}}{\left(1-2q\right)^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}\right|

(230)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-2q)^{2d}}\Bigg[\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}\right|\prod_{j=1}^{t}\left|\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}\right|\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}\right|\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{j}}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{j}}\right|\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}\left|\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{k}}\right|\Bigg]

(231)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}\right|}{(1-2q)^{2d_{0}}}\prod_{j=1}^{t}\left|\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}}{(1-2q)^{2d_{i}}}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}}{(1-2q)^{2t}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}\right|}{(1-2q)^{2(d_{i}+1)}}\frac{|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}|}{(1-2q)^{2d_{0}}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{j}}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{j}}\right|\prod_{k=i+1}^{t}\left|\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{k}}\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{k}}\right|}{(1-2q)^{2(d-d_{i}-d_{0}-1)}}

(232)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{\dagger}\left(\frac{\tau^{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}+\left(\frac{\tau^{\dagger}+\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}\right|}{(1-2q)^{2(d_{i}+1)}}

(233)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{\dagger}\left(\frac{\tau^{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}+\left(\frac{\tau^{\dagger}+\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left(\frac{\left|(\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}})\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}\right|}{(1-2q)^{2(d_{i}+1)}}+\frac{\left|\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}(\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{i}})\right|}{(1-2q)^{2(d_{i}+1)}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{\dagger}\left(\frac{\tau^{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}+\left(\frac{\tau^{\dagger}+\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}+\frac{\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)

(234)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\tau^{\dagger}+\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}(2t+1)\max\left\{\gamma_{\dagger},\frac{\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4\epsilon_{\dagger}e^{\beta}\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)+\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}(2t+1)\max\left\{\gamma_{\dagger},\frac{\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right\}

(235)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{5\epsilon_{\dagger}e^{\beta}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right)^{t-1}\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)^{t-1}(2t+1)\max\left\{\gamma_{\dagger},\frac{\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2t+1}{4^{t-1}}\frac{\eta}{3}

(236)

\displaystyle\leq\eta.

(237)

Telescoping summation and triangle inequality give (
230
) and (
231
). We use the definition of
d
,
d=\sum^{t}_{i=0}d_{i}+t
to get (
232
). The inequalities
\left|\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\right|\leq(1-2q)^{2d_{i}},

\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\right|\leq(1-2q)^{2d_{i}},

\left|\mu^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}\right|\leq\tau^{\dagger}
,

\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{e_{i}}\right|\leq\tau^{\dagger}+\epsilon_{\dagger}
hold under

\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{corr}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)
,
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{strong}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)
. Further, under event
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{cascade}}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}\right)
(Lemma
25
) it is true that
\left|\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}-\mu^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}\right|\leq\gamma_{\dagger}
, the latter give (
233
) and (
234
).
The bound
\tau^{\dagger}\leq 4\epsilon_{\dagger}e^{\beta}(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta})
gives (
235
) (see inequality
137
). Inequality (
236
) requires

\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}},\gamma_{\dagger}\right\}\leq\frac{\eta}{3}

(238)

and

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\dagger}\leq(1-2q)^{2}e^{-\beta}\left[20\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)\right]^{-1}.

(239)

Finally (
237
) holds for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
. The latter completes the proof.
∎

The next Lemma provides the set of values of
\epsilon_{\dagger}
that guarantee
\mathcal{L}^{(2)}(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)))\leq\frac{\eta}{2}
with high probability.

Lemma 27
.

If

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\dagger}\leq\min\left\{\frac{\eta}{16}(1-2q)^{2},\frac{(1-2q)^{2}e^{-\beta}}{24\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)}\right\}.

(240)

then
\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right)\right)\leq\frac{\eta}{2}
under the event
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{corr}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)\cap\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{strong}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)
.

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left|\prod_{e\in\text{path}_{\mathrm{T}}(w,\tilde{w})}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{e}}{(1-2q)^{2}}-\prod_{e\in\text{path}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(w,\tilde{w})}\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{e}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left|\prod_{e\in\text{path}_{\mathrm{T}}(w,\tilde{w})}\mu_{e}-\prod_{e\in\text{path}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(w,\tilde{w})}\mu_{e}\right|.

(241)

We follow Bresler’s and Karzand’s technique “Loss due to graph estimation”
(
Bresler and Karzand 2020
, Section 6.2)
and highlight the difference that appears in our setting. For the noisy case/hidden model, the argument changes slightly in the following manner:

\displaystyle 2\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq|\mu_{f}\mu_{A}\mu_{\tilde{A}}\mu_{B}\mu_{\tilde{B}}||\mu^{2}_{C}\mu^{2}_{\tilde{C}}-1|+|\mu_{f}|\left(\Delta(k)+\Delta(\tilde{k})+\Delta(\tilde{k})\Delta(k)\right)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{f}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\mu_{A}\mu_{\tilde{A}}\mu_{B}\mu_{\tilde{B}}\right|\left|\mu^{2}_{C}\mu^{2}_{\tilde{C}}-1\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left|\frac{\mu^{\dagger}_{f}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right|\left(\Delta(k)+\Delta(\tilde{k})+\Delta(\tilde{k})\Delta(k)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 8\frac{\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}+\frac{\tau^{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}(2\eta+\eta^{2})

(242)

\displaystyle\leq\eta.

(243)

(
242
) holds since
|\mu^{\dagger}_{f}|-|\mu^{\dagger}_{g}|\leq 4\epsilon_{\dagger}
,
|\mu_{f}|\left(1-\mu^{2}_{C}\mu^{2}_{\tilde{C}}\right)\leq 2|\mu_{f}|-2|\mu_{g}|
,
|\mu^{\dagger}_{f}|\leq\tau^{\dagger}
, and (
243
) holds for all the values of
\epsilon_{\dagger}
that satisfy

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\dagger}\leq\min\left\{\frac{\eta}{16}(1-2q)^{2},\frac{(1-2q)^{2}e^{-\beta}}{24\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)}\right\}.

(244)

The latter provides the statement of the Lemma.∎

The next Theorem provides the sufficient number of samples for predictive learning that recovers exactly the noiseless setting for
q=0
. Note that the dependence on
\beta
changes from
e^{2\beta}
to
e^{4\beta}
when the data are noisy. A key component of the bound is the following function

\displaystyle\Gamma(\beta,q)\triangleq\left(\frac{1-(1-2q)^{2}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\right)^{2},\quad\beta>0\text{ and }q\in[0,1/2).

(245)

Note that
\Gamma(\beta,q)\in[0,1]
for all
\beta>0
and
q\in[0,1/2)
, and
\Gamma(\beta,0)=0
for all
\beta>0
. Further we define

\displaystyle B(\beta,q)\triangleq\max\left\{\frac{1}{K(\beta,q)},\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)^{2}\right\},

(246)

and the expression of
K(\beta,q)
is given by (
168
).

Theorem 28
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
. Choose
\eta>0
(independent of
\delta
). If

\displaystyle n\geq

\displaystyle\max\Bigg\{\frac{512}{\eta^{2}(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{1152e^{2\beta}B(\beta,q)}{(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{48e^{4\beta}}{\eta^{2}}\Gamma(\beta,q)\Bigg\}\log\left(\frac{6p^{3}}{\delta}\right),

(247)

then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{p}_{\dagger})\right)\leq\eta\right)\geq 1-\delta.

(248)

Additionally, as a consequence of (
247
) , if

\displaystyle n\geq

\displaystyle\max\Bigg\{\frac{512}{\eta^{2}(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{1152\left(1+3\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}e^{2\beta(1+\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0})}}{(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{48e^{4\beta}}{\eta^{2}}\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}\Bigg\}\log\left(\frac{6p^{3}}{\delta}\right),

(249)

then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{p}_{\dagger})\right)\leq\eta\right)\geq 1-\delta.

(250)

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right)\right)=\frac{1}{2}\left|\prod_{e\in\text{path}_{\mathrm{T}}(w,\tilde{w})}\mu_{e}-\prod_{e\in\text{path}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(w,\tilde{w})}\mu_{e}\right|.

(251)

We combine the triangle inequality

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger})\right)\leq\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right)\right)+\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}\left(\mathrm{p}(\cdot)\right),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger})\right),

(252)

Lemma
26
, and Lemma
27
to get that
\mathcal{L}^{(2)}(\mathrm{p}(\cdot),\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger}))\leq\eta
with probability at least
1-\delta
if

\displaystyle\gamma_{\dagger}\leq\frac{\eta}{3}\text{ and }\epsilon_{\dagger}\leq\min\left\{\frac{\eta}{16}(1-2q)^{2},\frac{(1-2q)^{2}e^{-\beta}}{24\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)}\right\}.

(253)

First, we find the necessary number of samples such that for
\gamma_{\dagger}\leq\eta/3
the probability of the complement of
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{cascade}}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}\right)
is not greater than
\delta/3
. Recall that

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{3\left(3e^{-1}\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}+1\right)}{4(1-2q)^{2}},

(254)

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\triangleq 3-(1-2q)^{2}.

(255)

Recall that

\displaystyle\Gamma(\beta,q)\triangleq\left(\frac{1-(1-2q)^{2}}{1-(1-2q)^{4}\tanh^{2}(\beta)}\right)^{2},\quad\beta>0\text{ and }q\in[0,1/2).

(256)

Lemma
25
gives that for any
\Delta>0
and
\eta>\Delta
if

\displaystyle\hskip-9.10509ptn\geq\max\left\{\frac{0.3^{-2}S^{2}G^{2}}{(\eta-\Delta)^{2}},\frac{108e^{2\beta}}{(1-2q)^{4}K(\beta,q)},\frac{3e^{4\beta}}{\Delta^{2}}\Gamma(\beta,q)\right\}\log\left(\frac{6p^{3}}{\delta}\right),

(257)

then the probability of the complement of
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{cascade}}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}\right)
is upper bounded by
\delta/3
and we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{cascade}}\left(\gamma_{\dagger}\right)\right)^{c}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{3}.

(258)

Second, we find the necessary number of samples such that the complements of the events
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{strong}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)
and
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{corr}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)
occur with probability not greater than
\delta/3
each. In fact the upper bound on
\epsilon_{\dagger}
(
253
) and Lemma
18
gives that if

\displaystyle n\geq\max\left\{\frac{512}{\eta^{2}(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{1152e^{2\beta}}{(1-2q)^{4}}\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)^{2}\right\}\log\left(\frac{6p^{3}}{\delta}\right),

(259)

then
\epsilon_{\dagger}
satisfies the inequality in (
253
) with probability at least
1-\delta/3
. Note that (
257
) holds for any
\Delta\in(0,\eta)
and we will choose
\Delta=\eta/4
. Under the choice
\Delta=\eta/4

\displaystyle\frac{0.3^{-2}S^{2}G^{2}}{(\eta-\Delta)^{2}}=\frac{0.3^{-2}S^{2}G^{2}}{(\eta-\eta/4)^{2}}<\frac{512}{\eta^{2}},\quad\forall\eta>0,q\in[0,1/2).

(260)

Recall that

\displaystyle B(\beta,q)\triangleq\max\left\{\frac{1}{K(\beta,q)},\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)^{2}\right\}.

(261)

Combining (
257
), (
259
), (
260
) and (
261
) yields

\displaystyle n\geq

\displaystyle\max\Bigg\{\frac{512}{\eta^{2}(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{1152e^{2\beta}B(\beta,q)}{(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{48e^{4\beta}}{\eta^{2}}\Gamma(\beta,q)\Bigg\}\log\left(\frac{6p^{3}}{\delta}\right).

(262)

The latter gives the sample complexity for accurate predictive learning, it reduces exactly to the noiseless setting of prior work by
Bresler and Karzand 2020
and it is continuous because

\displaystyle\lim_{q\to 0^{+}}\Gamma(\beta,q)=\Gamma(\beta,0)=0,\quad\forall\beta>0

(263)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{q\to 0^{+}}K(\beta,q)=K(\beta,0)=1,\quad\forall\beta>0

(264)

\displaystyle\lim_{q\to 0^{+}}\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)^{2}=1

(265)

thus

\displaystyle\lim_{q\to 0^{+}}B(\beta,q)=\Gamma(\beta,0)=1,\quad\forall\beta>0.

(266)

To derive a simplified version of (
262
) note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K(\beta,q)}\leq e^{2\beta\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}}

(267)

by the definition (
168
) of
K(\beta,q)
and

\displaystyle\left(1+2e^{\beta}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(e^{\beta\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}}+2e^{\beta\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}}\sqrt{2\left(1-q\right)q\tanh\beta}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+3\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}e^{2\beta\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}}.

(268)

Then (
261
), (
267
) and (
268
) give

\displaystyle B(\beta,q)\leq\left(1+3\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}e^{2\beta\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}}

(269)

and by the definition (
256
)
\Gamma(\beta,q)\in[0,1)
and
\Gamma(\beta,0)=0
for all
\beta>0
, thus

\displaystyle\Gamma(\beta,q)\leq\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}.

(270)

Finally, we combine (
262
), (
269
), (
270
) to get

\displaystyle n\geq

\displaystyle\max\Bigg\{\frac{512}{\eta^{2}(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{1152\left(1+3\sqrt{q}\right)^{2}e^{2\beta(1+\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0})}}{(1-2q)^{4}},\frac{48e^{4\beta}}{\eta^{2}}\mathds{1}_{q\neq 0}\Bigg\}\log\left(\frac{6p^{3}}{\delta}\right).

(271)

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Theorem
11
: KL-Divergence Loss

Assume the Ising model tree distributions
\text{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
according
to a tree
\mathrm{T}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\left(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\right)

and the estimate
\text{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta^{\prime}}}
according a tree

\mathrm{T}_{\boldsymbol{\theta^{\prime}}}=\left(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}}\right)

The goal is to upper bound the symmetric KL divergence

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\theta_{st}-\theta^{\prime}_{st}\right)\left(\mu_{st}-\mu^{\prime}_{st}\right)

with high probability.
Under the event
\mathrm{E}^{\text{corr}}\left(\epsilon\right)
we can upper
bound the quantity
\left|\mu_{st}-\mu^{\prime}_{st}\right|
for all
(s,t)\in\mathcal{E}

with high probability.

By using bounds
\left|\theta_{st}-\theta^{\prime}_{st}\right|\leq 2\beta

and
\left|\mu_{st}-\mu^{\prime}_{st}\right|\leq\epsilon
for all
(s,t)\in\mathcal{E}

under the event
\mathrm{E}^{\text{corr}}\left(\epsilon\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\left|\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}||\boldsymbol{\theta}^{\prime}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\theta_{st}-\theta^{\prime}_{st}\right)\left(\mu_{st}-\mu^{\prime}_{st}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s,t\in\mathcal{E}}\left|\theta_{st}-\theta^{\prime}_{st}\right|\left|\mu_{st}-\mu^{\prime}_{st}\right|

\displaystyle\leq(p-1)\left|\beta-(-\beta)\right|\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{S},

(272)

by assuming
\epsilon\leq\frac{\eta_{s}}{2\beta(p-1)}
. The sufficient number of samples satisfies the inequality

\displaystyle n\geq 4\log\left(p^{2}/\delta\right)\frac{\beta^{2}(p-1)^{2}}{\eta_{s}^{2}}.

(273)

Now assume that
n_{\dagger}
samples of
\mathbf{Y}
are given, by using the estimate
\text{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta^{\prime}}}=\Pi{}_{\mathrm{T}^{\text{CL}}_{\dagger}}(\hat{\mathrm{p}}_{\dagger})
defined in (
227
) under the event
\mathrm{E}_{\dagger}^{\text{corr}}\left(\epsilon_{\dagger}\right)
we have
\left|\mu_{st}-\frac{\hat{\mu}^{\dagger}_{st}}{(1-2q)^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon_{\dagger}}{(1-2q)^{2}}
from Lemma
18
. In the same way by assuming
\epsilon_{\dagger}\leq\frac{\eta_{s}(1-2q)^{2}}{2\beta(p-1)}
, we get

\displaystyle n_{\dagger}\geq 4\log\left(p^{2}/\delta\right)\frac{\beta^{2}(p-1)^{2}}{(1-2q)^{4}\eta_{s}^{2}}.

(274)

Appendix G
Theorem
6
and Theorem
8
: Proofs

We combine Fano’s inequality and a Strong Data Processing Inequality to prove the necessary number of samples in the hidden model setting, first for structure learning (Theorem
6
) and then for inference (Theorem
8
). We use the following variation of Fano’s inequality.

Corollary 29
.

(
Tsybakov 2009
, Corollary 2.6)
:
Assume that
\Theta
is a family of
M+1
distributions
\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{M}
such that
M\geq 2
. Let
P_{\theta_{i}}
be the distribution of the variable
X
under the model
\theta_{i}
, if

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M+1}\sum^{M}_{i=1}\boldsymbol{D}_{\text{KL}}\left(P_{\theta_{i}}||P_{\theta_{0}}\right)\leq\gamma\log M,\quad\text{for }\gamma\in(0,1)

(275)

then for the probability of error
p_{e}
the following inequality holds:
p_{e}\geq\frac{\log(M+1)-\log(2)}{\log(M)}-\gamma
.

The construction from the noiseless case, with
Corollary
29
and the Strong Data Processing Inequality for the BSC yield the bound of Theorem
6
. We start by presenting Bresler’s and Karzand’s construction, which gives a sufficiently tight upper bound on symmetric KL divergence.

Proof of Theorem
6
:

Consider a family of
M+1
different Ising model distributions
\{P_{\theta^{i}}:i\in\{0,\ldots,M\}\}
. This family of the structured distributions is chosen such that the structure recovery task (through The Chow-Liu algorithm) is sufficiently hard. First, we define
P_{\theta^{0}}
to be an Ising model distribution with underlying structure a chain with
p
nodes and parameters
\theta^{0}_{j,j+1}=\alpha
, when
j
is odd and
\theta^{0}_{j,j+1}=\beta
when
j
is even. The rest of family is constructed as follows: the elements of each
\theta^{i}
,
i\in[M]
are equal to the elements of
\theta^{0}
apart from two elements
\theta^{i}_{i,i+1}=0
and
\theta^{i}_{i,i+2}=\arctanh(\tanh(\alpha)\tanh(\beta))
, for each odd value of
j
. There are
(p+1)/2
distinct distributions in the constructed family. Through the expression (
49
), we derive the following upper bound on the
\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}(P_{\theta^{0}}||P_{\theta^{i}})
, for all
i\in[M]
,
(
Bresler and Karzand 2020
, Section 7.1)
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}(P_{\theta^{0}}||P_{\theta^{i}})=\alpha\left(\tanh(\alpha)-\tanh(\alpha)\tanh^{2}(\beta)\right)\leq 4\alpha\tanh(\alpha)e^{-2\beta}.

(276)

Strong Data Processing Inequality
:
For each distribution
P_{\theta^{i}}
and
i\in\{0,\ldots,M\}
we consider the distribution of the noisy variable in the hidden model
P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{i}}\triangleq P_{\mathbf{Y}|\mathbf{X}}\circ P_{\theta^{i}}
. We would like to find an upper bound for the quantities
\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}(P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{0}}||P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{i}})
,
i\in\{0,\ldots,M\}
. For that purpose, we a use a strong data processing inequality result for the BSC by
Polyanskiy and Wu 2017
. The input random variable
\mathbf{X}
is considered to have correlated binary elements, while the noise variables
N_{i}
are i.i.d
\mathrm{Rademacher}(q)
. This scheme is equivalent to the hidden model that we consider in this paper. In fact we have the following bound

\displaystyle\eta_{\text{KL}}\leq 1-(4q(1-q))^{p},

(277)

that is proved by Polyanski
(
Polyanskiy and Wu 2017
, “Evaluation for the BSC”, equation (39))
, where the quantity
\eta_{\text{KL}}
is defined as

\displaystyle\eta_{\text{KL}}\triangleq\sup_{Q}\sup_{P:0<\boldsymbol{D}_{\text{KL}}(P||Q)<\infty}\frac{\boldsymbol{D}_{\text{KL}}\left(P_{\mathbf{Y}|\mathbf{X}}\circ P||P_{\mathbf{Y}|\mathbf{X}}\circ Q\right)}{\boldsymbol{D}_{\text{KL}}\left(P||Q\right)},

(278)

P_{\mathbf{Y}|\mathbf{X}}
is the distribution of the BSC and
P,Q
are any distributions of the input variable
\mathbf{X}
.

Since the supremum in (
278
) is with respect to all possible distributions, it covers any pair of distributions in the desired family
\{P_{\theta^{j}}:j\in\{0,\ldots,M\}\}
. Thus, for all
k,\ell\in\{0,1,\ldots,M\}
and
k\neq\ell
, it is true that

\displaystyle\frac{\boldsymbol{D}_{\text{KL}}(P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{k}}||P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{\ell}})}{\boldsymbol{D}_{\text{KL}}(P_{\theta^{k}}||P_{\theta^{\ell}})}

\displaystyle\leq 1-(4q(1-q))^{p},

(279)

which comes from (
277
),(
278
) and implies the following

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}(P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{k}}||P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{\ell}})

\displaystyle\leq[1-(4q(1-q))^{p}]\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}(P_{\theta^{k}}||P_{\theta^{\ell}}),\quad\forall k\neq\ell\in\{0,1,\ldots,M\}.

(280)

We combine (
276
) and (
280
) to get

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}(P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{k}}||P^{\dagger}_{\theta^{i}})

\displaystyle\leq[1-(4q(1-q))^{p}]4\alpha\tanh(\alpha)e^{-2\beta}\leq[1-(4q(1-q))^{p}]4\alpha^{2}e^{-2\beta}.

(281)

Finally, from (
281
) and Corollary
29
we derive the first part of Theorem
6
.

Proof of Theorem
8
: Theorem
8
is the extended version of Theorem 3.4 by
Bresler and Karzand 2020
to the hidden model. Following a similar technique, we consider chain structured Ising models with parameters
\theta^{j}
for
j\in[M]
such that
\theta^{j}_{j,j+1}=\alpha
and
\theta^{j}_{i,i+1}=\arctanh(\tanh(\alpha)+2\eta)
, for all
i\neq j
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(2)}\left(P_{\theta^{j}},P_{\theta^{j^{\prime}}}\right)=\max_{s,t}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{j}}[X_{s}X_{t}]-\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{j^{\prime}}}[X_{s}X_{t}]\right|\geq 2\eta

(282)

and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{KL}}\left(P_{\theta^{j}},P_{\theta^{j^{\prime}}}\right)\leq 2\eta\left[\arctanh(\tanh(\alpha)+2\eta)-\alpha\right]\leq 2\eta\frac{2\eta}{1-\left[\tanh(\alpha)+2\eta\right]^{2}},

(283)

where the last inequality is a consequence of Mean Value Theorem (see
(
Bresler and Karzand 2020
, Section 6.3)
for the original statement). We derive the bound of Theorem
8
by combining the strong data processing inequality (
278
) with (
277
), (
283
), and Corollary
29
.

Appendix H
Supplementary Discussion

In this section we provide supplementary material that supports the discussion in Sections
2.4
and
2.6
. First, we present one marginal case for which perfect denoising is possible before applying the Chow-Liu algorithm. Then we show a structure-preserving case.

H.1
The Gap between the Upper and Lower Bounds

We continue by analyzing the gap that appears between the upper and lower bounds for an example where perfect denoising can be applied on a specific class of tree models in the high-dimensional regime. This shows why the effect of noise vanishes in Theorems 1.2 and 1.4 for
p\to\infty
. Further, while it seems counter-intuitive that when
p\to\infty
the problem becomes easier, we show below one example that this is the case. Our lower bound is directly affected by marginal cases like this, for instance see Proposition 1.4.

The gap is introduced by the terms
(1-2q)^{4}
and
1-(4q(1-2q))^{p}
in the denominator of the lower and upper bounds respectively. Specifically, for
p\to\infty
there exists a special case for which perfect denoising before running the Chow-Liu algorithm is possible, while in other cases that is not possible. Thus the minimax bound ought to be identical to noiseless case when
p\to\infty
and
1-(4q(1-2q))^{p}\to 1
in the large dimensional regime. We continue by providing the marginal case of a trivial tree structure and showing that perfect denoising is possible in this case before running the Chow-Liu algorithm.

First notice that if
p\to\infty
, then the sample size
n\to\infty
, even in the noiseless regime. Consider the case of
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}X_{j}]\to 1
for all
(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. Because an infinite number of samples are available, we can estimate perfectly the correlations of the observables and we find
\hat{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{i}Y_{j}]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}Y_{j}]=(1-2q)^{2}
for all
(i,j)\in\mathcal{V}
. The latter as information is sufficient to find that
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}X_{j}]\to 1
for all
(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. The hidden layer
\mathbf{X}
take two values,
(+1,+1,\ldots)\triangleq+1^{p}
(
p
values
+1
) or
(-1.-1,\ldots)\triangleq-1^{p}
(
p
values
-1
), because
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}X_{j}]\to 1
for all
(i,j)\in\mathcal{V}
and the later allows us to denoise each sample. Define as
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})
the Hamming distance between
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{Y}
. At this point we can perform perfect denoising for each sample
\mathbf{y}_{s}
of infinite length
p
and find the hidden sample
\mathbf{x}_{s}
with probability
1
because

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{x}_{s}|\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{y}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{y}_{s}|\mathbf{X}_{s}=\mathbf{x}_{s}\right)\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{X}_{s}=\mathbf{x}_{s})}{\sum_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{y}_{s}|\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{x}\right)\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{x})}

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}(1-q)^{p-\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}}{q^{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}(1-q)^{p-\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}+q^{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(-\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}(1-q)^{p-\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(-\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}}

(284)

and the last holds for both of the cases
\mathbf{x}_{s}=+1^{p}
or
\mathbf{x}_{s}=-1^{p}
because of symmetry. Further for any observation
\mathbf{y}_{s}
for any
q\in(0,1/2)
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(-\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})-\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}{p}\overset{a.s.}{=}1-q-q=1-2q.

(285)

We combine (
284
) and (
285
) to find

\displaystyle\lim_{p\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{x}_{s}|\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{y}_{s}\right)=\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{1+\left(\frac{q}{1-q}\right)^{\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(-\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})-\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}{p}p}}=1

(286)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{p\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{X}=-\mathbf{x}_{s}|\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{y}_{s}\right)=\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{1+\left(\frac{1-q}{q}\right)^{\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(-\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})-\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{s},\mathbf{y}_{s})}{p}p}}=0.

(287)

As a consequence there exists one case for which perfect denoising is possible before running the Chow-Liu algorithm. Because we want Theorems 1.2 and 1.4 to reduce to the noiseless case for
p\to\infty
, the above best case scenario must be covered. However, perfect denoising is not possible in general (for instance
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}X_{j}]<1
and finite
p
.).

H.2
A Structure-Preserving Case

Lemma
30
considers a special case of tree structures for the hidden variables, the set of edges is a set with disconnected edges, no edge is connected to any other. Then we show that the same structure is preserved for the observable variables.

Lemma 30
.

Let
\mathrm{F}=(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{E})
be a forest with
|\mathcal{V}|=p
and
|\mathcal{E}|=p/2\in\mathbb{N}
such that no edge is connected to any other edge. Assume that
X_{i}\in\{-1,+1\}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}\right]=0
for all
i\in[1,\ldots,p]
. If
\mathbf{Y}
is the output of the BSC channel (in the hidden model) with distribution
\mathrm{p}_{\dagger}(\mathbf{y})
, then
\mathrm{p}_{\dagger}(\mathbf{y})
also factorizes with respect to
\mathrm{F}
.

Proof.

The pair variables
(Y_{i},Y_{j})
for
(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}
are independent because of the disconnected edges of the hidden layer. The latter directly gives the factorization as

\displaystyle\mathrm{p}_{\dagger}(\mathbf{y})=\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{p}_{\dagger}(y_{i},y_{j})=\prod_{i\in V}\mathrm{p}_{\dagger}\left(y_{i}\right)\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\frac{\mathrm{p}_{\dagger}(y_{i},y_{j})}{\mathrm{p}_{\dagger}(y_{i})\mathrm{p}_{\dagger}(y_{j})},

(288)

because
|\mathcal{V}|=p
,
|\mathcal{E}|=p/2
and the marginal distributions are uniform.
∎
